\displaystyle\beta_{\Pi}=15\Big(\frac{1}{3}-c^{2}_{s}\Big)^{2}\big(\mathcal{E}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}\big)+\mathcal{O}(z^{5})\,,

(B.40b)

\displaystyle\delta_{\Pi\Pi}=\frac{2}{3}+\mathcal{O}(z^{2}\ln z)\,,

(B.40c)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\Pi\pi}=\frac{8}{5}\Big(\frac{1}{3}-c^{2}_{s}\Big)+\mathcal{O}(z^{4})\,,

(B.40d)

\displaystyle\tau_{\pi\pi}=\frac{10}{7}+\mathcal{O}(z^{2})\,,

(B.40e)

\displaystyle\delta_{\pi\pi}=\frac{4}{3}+\mathcal{O}(z^{2})\,,

(B.40f)

\displaystyle\lambda_{\pi\Pi}=\frac{6}{5}+\mathcal{O}(z^{2}\ln z)\,.

(B.40g)

Note that both viscous hydrodynamic models use the same shear and bulk viscosities as anisotropic hydrodynamics (e.g. Figs.
3.2
and
4.3
).

For Bjorken flow, the energy conservation law (
B.26
) and relaxation equations (
B.38
) simplify greatly. The gradient terms are
\sigma^{\mu\nu}=\mathrm{diag}(0,\frac{1}{3\tau},\frac{1}{3\tau},-\frac{2}{3\tau^{3}})
,
\theta=1/\tau
, and
\omega^{\mu\nu}=0
. The shear stress components are
\pi^{\mu\nu}=\mathrm{diag}(0,-\frac{1}{2}\tau^{2}\pi^{\eta\eta},-\frac{1}{2}\tau^{2}\pi^{\eta\eta},\pi^{\eta\eta})
. As a result, the viscous hydrodynamic equations reduce to (
3.59
).

Appendix C

C.1
Gradient source terms

In this appendix, we list the gradients that appear in the source terms of the anisotropic hydrodynamic equations. The derivatives of the fluid velocity component
u^{\tau}
are

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}u^{\tau}

\displaystyle=v^{x}\partial_{\tau}u^{x}+v^{y}\partial_{\tau}u^{y}+\tau^{2}v^{\eta}\partial_{\tau}u^{\eta}+\tau v^{\eta}u^{\eta}\,,

(C.1a)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}u^{\tau}

\displaystyle=v^{x}\partial_{x}u^{x}+v^{y}\partial_{x}u^{y}+\tau^{2}v^{\eta}\partial_{x}u^{\eta}\,,

(C.1b)

\displaystyle\partial_{y}u^{\tau}

\displaystyle=v^{x}\partial_{y}u^{x}+v^{y}\partial_{y}u^{y}+\tau^{2}v^{\eta}\partial_{y}u^{\eta}\,,

(C.1c)

\displaystyle\partial_{\eta}u^{\tau}

\displaystyle=v^{x}\partial_{\eta}u^{x}+v^{y}\partial_{\eta}u^{y}+\tau^{2}v^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}u^{\eta}\,.

(C.1d)

The derivatives of the longitudinal basis vector
z^{\mu}
are

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}z^{\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{\sqrt{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}}\left(\partial_{\tau}u^{\eta}-\frac{u^{\eta}\left(u^{x}\partial_{\tau}u^{x}{+}u^{y}\partial_{\tau}u^{y}\right)}{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}\right)+\frac{z^{\tau}}{\tau}\,,

(C.2a)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}z^{\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{\sqrt{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}}\left(\partial_{x}u^{\eta}-\frac{u^{\eta}\left(u^{x}\partial_{x}u^{x}{+}u^{y}\partial_{x}u^{y}\right)}{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\,,

(C.2b)

\displaystyle\partial_{y}z^{\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{\sqrt{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}}\left(\partial_{y}u^{\eta}-\frac{u^{\eta}\left(u^{x}\partial_{y}u^{x}{+}u^{y}\partial_{y}u^{y}\right)}{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\,,

(C.2c)

\displaystyle\partial_{\eta}z^{\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{\sqrt{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}}\left(\partial_{\eta}u^{\eta}-\frac{u^{\eta}\left(u^{x}\partial_{\eta}u^{x}{+}u^{y}\partial_{\eta}u^{y}\right)}{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\,,

(C.2d)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}z^{\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau\sqrt{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}}\left(\partial_{\tau}u^{\tau}-\frac{u^{\tau}\left(u^{x}\partial_{\tau}u^{x}{+}u^{y}\partial_{\tau}u^{y}\right)}{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}\right)-\frac{z^{\eta}}{\tau}\,,

(C.2e)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}z^{\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau\sqrt{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}}\left(\partial_{x}u^{\tau}-\frac{u^{\tau}\left(u^{x}\partial_{x}u^{x}{+}u^{y}\partial_{x}u^{y}\right)}{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\,,

(C.2f)

\displaystyle\partial_{y}z^{\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau\sqrt{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}}\left(\partial_{y}u^{\tau}-\frac{u^{\tau}\left(u^{x}\partial_{y}u^{x}{+}u^{y}\partial_{y}u^{y}\right)}{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\,,

(C.2g)

\displaystyle\partial_{\eta}z^{\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\tau\sqrt{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}}\left(\partial_{\eta}u^{\tau}-\frac{u^{\tau}\left(u^{x}\partial_{\eta}u^{x}{+}u^{y}\partial_{\eta}u^{y}\right)}{1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\,.

(C.2h)

The divergence of the spatial fluid velocity
v^{i}=u^{i}/u^{\tau}
is

\partial_{i}v^{i}=\frac{\partial_{x}u^{x}-v^{x}\partial_{x}u^{\tau}+\partial_{y}u^{y}-v^{y}\partial_{y}u^{\tau}+\partial_{\eta}u^{\eta}-v^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}u^{\tau}}{u^{\tau}}\,.

(C.3)

The longitudinal expansion rate is

\begin{split}\theta_{L}&=z_{\mu}D_{z}u^{\mu}=-z_{\mu}z^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}u^{\mu}-z_{\mu}z^{\nu}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\nu\lambda}u^{\lambda}\\
&=-(z^{\tau})^{2}\partial_{\tau}u^{\tau}+z^{\tau}z^{\eta}(\tau^{2}\partial_{\tau}u^{\eta}-\partial_{\eta}u^{\tau})+(\tau z^{\eta})^{2}\partial_{\eta}u^{\eta}+\tau(z^{\eta})^{2}u^{\tau}\,.\end{split}

(C.4)

The transverse expansion rate is

\theta_{\perp}=\nabla_{\perp\mu}u^{\mu}=\theta-\theta_{L}\,,

(C.5)

where

\begin{split}\theta&=D_{\mu}u^{\mu}=\partial_{\mu}u^{\mu}+\Gamma^{\mu}_{\mu\nu}u^{\nu}=\partial_{\tau}u^{\tau}+\partial_{x}u^{x}+\partial_{y}u^{y}+\partial_{\eta}u^{\eta}+\frac{u^{\tau}}{\tau}\end{split}

(C.6)

is the scalar expansion rate.

The components of the fluid acceleration

a^{\mu}=Du^{\mu}=u^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}u^{\mu}+u^{\nu}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\nu\lambda}u^{\lambda}

(C.7)

are

\displaystyle a^{\tau}

\displaystyle=u^{\tau}\partial_{\tau}u^{\tau}+u^{x}\partial_{x}u^{\tau}+u^{y}\partial_{y}u^{\tau}+u^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}u^{\tau}+\tau(u^{\eta})^{2}\,,

(C.8a)

\displaystyle a^{x}

\displaystyle=u^{\tau}\partial_{\tau}u^{x}+u^{x}\partial_{x}u^{x}+u^{y}\partial_{y}u^{x}+u^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}u^{x}\,,

(C.8b)

\displaystyle a^{y}

\displaystyle=u^{\tau}\partial_{\tau}u^{y}+u^{x}\partial_{x}u^{y}+u^{y}\partial_{y}u^{y}+u^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}u^{y}\,,

(C.8c)

\displaystyle a^{\eta}

\displaystyle=u^{\tau}\partial_{\tau}u^{\eta}+u^{x}\partial_{x}u^{\eta}+u^{y}\partial_{y}u^{\eta}+u^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}u^{\eta}+\frac{2u^{\tau}u^{\eta}}{\tau}\,.

(C.8d)

The components of the longitudinal vector’s co-moving time derivative

\dot{z}^{\mu}=Dz^{\mu}=u^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}z^{\mu}+u^{\nu}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\nu\lambda}z^{\lambda}

(C.9)

are

\displaystyle\dot{z}^{\tau}

\displaystyle=u^{\tau}\partial_{\tau}z^{\tau}+u^{x}\partial_{x}z^{\tau}+u^{y}\partial_{y}z^{\tau}+u^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}z^{\tau}+\tau u^{\eta}z^{\eta}\,,

(C.10a)

\displaystyle\dot{z}^{\eta}

\displaystyle=u^{\tau}\partial_{\tau}z^{\eta}+u^{x}\partial_{x}z^{\eta}+u^{y}\partial_{y}z^{\eta}+u^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}z^{\eta}+\frac{u^{\tau}z^{\eta}{+}u^{\eta}z^{\tau}}{\tau}\,.

(C.10b)

The components of the fluid velocity’s longitudinal derivative

D_{z}u^{\mu}=-z^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}u^{\mu}-z^{\nu}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\nu\lambda}u^{\lambda}

(C.11)

are

\displaystyle D_{z}u^{\tau}

\displaystyle=-z^{\tau}\partial_{\tau}u^{\tau}-z^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}u^{\tau}-\tau u^{\eta}z^{\eta}\,,

(C.12a)

\displaystyle D_{z}u^{x}

\displaystyle=-z^{\tau}\partial_{\tau}u^{x}-z^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}u^{x}\,,

(C.12b)

\displaystyle D_{z}u^{y}

\displaystyle=-z^{\tau}\partial_{\tau}u^{y}-z^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}u^{y}\,,

(C.12c)

\displaystyle D_{z}u^{\eta}

\displaystyle=-z^{\tau}\partial_{\tau}u^{\eta}-z^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}u^{\eta}-\frac{u^{\tau}z^{\eta}{+}u^{\eta}z^{\tau}}{\tau}\,.

(C.12d)

The transverse gradient of the fluid velocity projected along the longitudinal direction is

z_{\nu}\nabla_{\perp}^{\mu}u^{\nu}=\Xi^{\mu}_{\alpha}z_{\nu}D^{\alpha}u^{\nu}\,,

(C.13)

where the components of

z_{\nu}D^{\alpha}u^{\nu}=g^{\alpha\beta}z_{\nu}\partial_{\beta}u^{\nu}+g^{\alpha\beta}z_{\nu}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\beta\lambda}u^{\lambda}

(C.14)

are

\displaystyle z_{\nu}D^{\tau}u^{\nu}

\displaystyle=z^{\tau}\partial_{\tau}u^{\tau}-\tau^{2}z^{\eta}\partial_{\tau}u^{\eta}-\tau u^{\eta}z^{\eta}\,,

(C.15a)

\displaystyle z_{\nu}D^{x}u^{\nu}

\displaystyle=-z^{\tau}\partial_{x}u^{\tau}+\tau^{2}z^{\eta}\partial_{x}u^{\eta}\,,

(C.15b)

\displaystyle z_{\nu}D^{y}u^{\nu}

\displaystyle=-z^{\tau}\partial_{y}u^{\tau}+\tau^{2}z^{\eta}\partial_{y}u^{\eta}\,,

(C.15c)

\displaystyle z_{\nu}D^{\eta}u^{\nu}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}\left(z^{\tau}\partial_{\eta}u^{\tau}-\tau^{2}z^{\eta}\partial_{\eta}u^{\eta}+\tau\left(u^{\eta}z^{\tau}{-}u^{\tau}z^{\eta}\right)\right)

(C.15d)

The transverse velocity-shear tensor is
1
1

1

In the code we obtain
\sigma_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}
by applying the projector
\Xi^{\mu\nu}_{\alpha\beta}
onto
D^{(\alpha}u^{\beta)}
directly, rather than simplifying the expression (
C.16
).

\sigma_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}=\Xi^{\mu\nu}_{\alpha\beta}D^{(\alpha}u^{\beta)}\,,

(C.16)

where the components of

D^{(\alpha}u^{\beta)}=\frac{1}{2}\left(g^{\alpha\rho}\partial_{\rho}u^{\beta}+g^{\beta\rho}\partial_{\rho}u^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\rho}\Gamma^{\beta}_{\rho\lambda}u^{\lambda}+g^{\beta\rho}\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\rho\lambda}u^{\lambda}\right)

(C.17)

are

\displaystyle D^{(\tau}u^{\tau)}

\displaystyle=\partial_{\tau}u^{\tau}\,,

(C.18a)

\displaystyle D^{(\tau}u^{x)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{\tau}u^{x}-\partial_{x}u^{\tau}\right)\,,

(C.18b)

\displaystyle D^{(\tau}u^{y)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{\tau}u^{y}-\partial_{y}u^{\tau}\right)\,,

(C.18c)

\displaystyle D^{(\tau}u^{\eta)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{\tau}u^{\eta}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}u^{\tau}}{\tau^{2}}\right)\,,

(C.18d)

\displaystyle D^{(x}u^{x)}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{x}u^{x}\,,

(C.18e)

\displaystyle D^{(x}u^{y)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{x}u^{y}+\partial_{y}u^{x}\right)\,,

(C.18f)

\displaystyle D^{(x}u^{\eta)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{x}u^{\eta}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}u^{x}}{\tau^{2}}\right)\,,

(C.18g)

\displaystyle D^{(y}u^{y)}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{y}u^{y}\,,

(C.18h)

\displaystyle D^{(y}u^{\eta)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{y}u^{\eta}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}u^{y}}{\tau^{2}}\right)\,,

(C.18i)

\displaystyle D^{(\eta}u^{\eta)}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{\tau^{2}}\left(\partial_{\eta}u^{\eta}+\frac{u^{\tau}}{\tau}\right)\,.

(C.18j)

The transverse vorticity tensor is

\begin{split}\omega_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}&=\Xi^{\mu}_{\alpha}\Xi^{\nu}_{\beta}D^{[\alpha}u^{\beta]}\\
&=D^{[\mu}u^{\nu]}-\frac{u^{\mu}a^{\nu}{-}u^{\nu}a^{\mu}{+}z^{\mu}(D_{z}u^{\nu}{+}z_{\alpha}D^{\nu}u^{\alpha}){-}z^{\nu}(D_{z}u^{\mu}{+}z_{\alpha}D^{\mu}u^{\alpha})}{2}\end{split}

(C.19)

where the components of

D^{[\alpha}u^{\beta]}=\frac{1}{2}\left(g^{\alpha\rho}\partial_{\rho}u^{\beta}-g^{\beta\rho}\partial_{\rho}u^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\rho}\Gamma^{\beta}_{\rho\lambda}u^{\lambda}-g^{\beta\rho}\Gamma^{\alpha}_{\rho\lambda}u^{\lambda}\right)

(C.20)

are

\displaystyle D^{[\tau}u^{\tau]}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(C.21a)

\displaystyle D^{[\tau}u^{x]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{\tau}u^{x}+\partial_{x}u^{\tau}\right)\,,

(C.21b)

\displaystyle D^{[\tau}u^{y]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{\tau}u^{y}+\partial_{y}u^{\tau}\right)\,,

(C.21c)

\displaystyle D^{[\tau}u^{\eta]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{\tau}u^{\eta}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}u^{\tau}}{\tau^{2}}\right)+\frac{u^{\eta}}{\tau}\,,

(C.21d)

\displaystyle D^{[x}u^{x]}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(C.21e)

\displaystyle D^{[x}u^{y]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{x}u^{y}-\partial_{y}u^{x}\right)\,,

(C.21f)

\displaystyle D^{[x}u^{\eta]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{x}u^{\eta}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}u^{x}}{\tau^{2}}\right)\,,

(C.21g)

\displaystyle D^{[y}u^{y]}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(C.21h)

\displaystyle D^{[y}u^{\eta]}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\left(\partial_{y}u^{\eta}+\frac{\partial_{\eta}u^{y}}{\tau^{2}}\right)\,,

(C.21i)

\displaystyle D^{[\eta}u^{\eta]}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(C.21j)

C.2
Geometric source terms

Here we list the components of the geometric source terms
\mathcal{G}_{W}^{\mu}
and
\mathcal{G}_{\pi}^{\mu\nu}
(Eq. (
4.21
)) that appear in the relaxation equations (
4.18
) for
W^{\mu}_{\perp z}
and
\pi^{\mu\nu}_{\perp}
, respectively:

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{W}^{\tau}

\displaystyle=\tau u^{\eta}W_{\perp z}^{\eta}\,,

(C.22a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{W}^{x}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(C.22b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{W}^{y}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(C.22c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{W}^{\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{u^{\tau}W_{\perp z}^{\eta}+u^{\eta}W_{\perp z}^{\tau}}{\tau}\,.

(C.22d)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pi}^{\tau\tau}

\displaystyle=2\tau u^{\eta}\pi_{\perp}^{\tau\eta}\,,

(C.23a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pi}^{\tau x}

\displaystyle=\tau u^{\eta}\pi_{\perp}^{x\eta}\,,

(C.23b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pi}^{\tau y}

\displaystyle=\tau u^{\eta}\pi_{\perp}^{y\eta}\,,

(C.23c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pi}^{\tau\eta}

\displaystyle=\tau u^{\eta}\pi_{\perp}^{\eta\eta}+\frac{u^{\tau}\pi_{\perp}^{\tau\eta}{+}u^{\eta}\pi_{\perp}^{\tau\tau}}{\tau}\,,

(C.23d)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pi}^{xx}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(C.23e)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pi}^{xy}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(C.23f)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pi}^{x\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{u^{\tau}\pi_{\perp}^{x\eta}+u^{\eta}\pi_{\perp}^{\tau x}}{\tau}\,,

(C.23g)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pi}^{yy}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(C.23h)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pi}^{y\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{u^{\tau}\pi_{\perp}^{y\eta}+u^{\eta}\pi_{\perp}^{\tau y}}{\tau}\,,

(C.23i)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\pi}^{\eta\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\left(u^{\tau}\pi_{\perp}^{\eta\eta}+u^{\eta}\pi_{\perp}^{\tau\eta}\right)}{\tau}\,.

(C.23j)

C.3
Conformal anisotropic transport coefficients

In the conformal limit
m=B=0
, the anisotropic transport coefficients (
4.29
) – (
4.32
) only depend on the functions
\mathcal{I}_{nrqs}
given in Eq. (
B.1
), which reduce to

\mathcal{I}_{nrqs}=\frac{g(n{+}s{+}1)!\,\alpha_{L}^{r+1}\Lambda^{n+s+2}\mathcal{R}_{nrq}}{4\pi^{2}(2q)!!}\,,

(C.24)

where
g
,
\alpha_{L}
and
\Lambda
are given by Eqs. (
3.40
), (
4.69
) and (
4.70
), respectively. We list the functions
\mathcal{R}_{nrq}
used in Chapter
4

[
120
]
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{200}

\displaystyle=\alpha_{L}\big(1+(1+\xi_{L})t_{L}\big)\,,

(C.25a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{220}

\displaystyle=\frac{-1+(1+\xi_{L})t_{L}}{\xi_{L}\alpha_{L}}\,,

(C.25b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{201}

\displaystyle=\frac{1+(\xi_{L}-1)t_{L}}{\xi_{L}\alpha_{L}}\,,

(C.25c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{240}

\displaystyle=\frac{3+2\xi_{L}-3(1+\xi_{L})t_{L})}{\xi_{L}^{2}\alpha_{L}^{3}}\,,

(C.25d)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{202}

\displaystyle=\frac{3+\xi_{L}+(1+\xi_{L})(\xi_{L}-3)t_{L}}{\xi_{L}^{2}(1+\xi_{L})\alpha_{L}^{3}}\,,

(C.25e)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{221}

\displaystyle=\frac{-3+(3+\xi_{L})t_{L})}{\xi_{L}^{2}\alpha_{L}^{3}}\,,

(C.25f)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{441}

\displaystyle=\frac{-15+13\xi_{L}+3(1+\xi_{L})(5+\xi_{L})t_{L}}{4\xi_{L}^{3}\alpha_{L}^{3}}\,,

(C.25g)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{402}

\displaystyle=\frac{3(\xi_{L}-1)+(\xi_{L}(3\xi_{L}-2)+3)t_{L}}{4\xi_{L}^{2}\alpha_{L}}\,,

(C.25h)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{421}

\displaystyle=\frac{3+\xi_{L}+(1+\xi_{L})(\xi_{L}-3)t_{L}}{4\xi_{L}^{2}\alpha_{L}}\,,

(C.25i)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{422}

\displaystyle=\frac{15+\xi_{L}+(\xi_{L}(\xi_{L}-6)-15)t_{L}}{4\xi_{L}^{3}\alpha_{L}^{3}}\,,

(C.25j)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}_{403}

\displaystyle=\frac{(\xi_{L}-3)(5+3\xi_{L})+3(1+\xi_{L})(\xi_{L}(\xi_{L}-2)+5)t_{L}}{4\xi_{L}^{3}(1+\xi_{L})\alpha_{L}^{3}}\,,

(C.25k)

where
\xi_{L}=\alpha_{L}^{-2}-1
and
t_{L}=\mathrm{arctan}\sqrt{\xi_{L}}/\sqrt{\xi_{L}}
. These are the same functions as Eq. (
B.6
) except here we set
\alpha_{\perp}=1
; this translates to
w=\alpha_{L}
and
z=\xi_{L}
.

C.4
T
{}_{\text{\sc R}}
ENTo
energy deposition model

In the
T
{}_{\text{\sc R}}
ENTo
model, the transverse energy deposition (GeV/fm
2
) of a single fluctuating nuclear collision event in the mid-rapidity region is
[
42
]

\frac{dE_{T}}{dxdyd\eta_{s}}_{\big|{\eta_{s}=0}}=N\times T_{R}(x,y)\,,

(C.26)

where
N
is the normalization parameter and

T_{R}(x,y)=\left(\frac{T^{p}_{A}(x,y)+T^{p}_{B}(x,y)}{2}\right)^{1/p}

(C.27)

is the reduced nuclear thickness function, with
p
being the geometric parameter. The nuclear thickness function of nucleus
A
(
B
) is
2
2

2

In Chapters
4
and
7
, we only consider Pb+Pb collisions (
A=B=208
) at LHC energies
\sqrt{s_{\text{NN}}}=2.76
TeV; the inelastic nucleon–nucleon cross section is set to
\sigma_{\text{NN}}=6.4
fm
-2
.

T_{A,B}(x,y)=\sum_{n=1}^{N_{\text{part},A,B}}\gamma_{n}\,T_{p}(x-x_{n},y-y_{n})\,,

(C.28)

where
N_{\text{part},A,B}
are the number of participant nucleons from nucleus
A
(
B
); the nucleon positions are sampled from a Woods–Saxon distribution under the constraint that each nucleon–nucleon pair in nucleus
A
(
B
) maintains a minimum separation
d_{\text{min}}

[
42
]
. The participant nucleon’s thickness function is centered around its sampled transverse position (
x_{n}
,
y_{n}
):

T_{p}(x-x_{n},y-y_{n})=\frac{1}{2\pi w^{2}}\times\exp\left[-\frac{(x{-}x_{n})^{2}+(y{-}y_{n})^{2}}{2w^{2}}\right]\,,

(C.29)

where
w
is the nucleon width parameter.
3
3

3

This parameter does not control the nucleon’s charge radius but rather the spread of thermal energy it deposits in the collision zone along the transverse directions.
Furthermore, the multiplicity factor
\gamma_{n}
of each participant nucleon is sampled from the gamma distribution

P(\gamma)=\frac{k^{k}\gamma^{k-1}\exp\left[-k\gamma\right]}{\Gamma[k]}\,,

(C.30)

where
k=\sigma_{k}^{-2}
and
\sigma_{k}
is the standard deviation.

For a very brief period
\tau_{0}
after the collision, we assume the system is longitudinally free-streaming and static in Milne coordinates (i.e.
\mathcal{P}_{L}/\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}\sim 0
and
\bm{u}\sim\bm{0}
) so that
\mathcal{E}(x)\propto 1/\tau_{0}
. Therefore, we initialize the energy density profile of the hydrodynamic simulation at the starting time
\tau_{0}
as

\mathcal{E}(\tau_{0},x,y,\eta_{s})=\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\times\frac{dE_{T}}{dxdyd\eta_{s}}_{\big|\eta_{s}=0}\times f_{L}(\eta_{s})\,.

(C.31)

In Chapter
4
we extend the transverse energy density profile along the spacetime rapidity direction with a smooth plateau distribution (unitless)
[
86
]
:

f_{L}(\eta_{s})=\exp\left[-\frac{\big(|\eta_{s}|-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\text{flat}}\big)^{2}\,\Theta\big(|\eta_{s}|-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{\text{flat}}\big)}{2\sigma_{\eta}^{2}}\right]\,,

(C.32)

where
\eta_{\text{flat}}
is the plateau length,
\sigma_{\eta}
is the standard deviation of the half-Gaussian tails and
\Theta
is the Heaviside step function. The initial condition parameter values used in Chapter
4
are
N=14.19
GeV,
p=0.06
,
w=1.11
fm,
d_{\text{min}}=1.45
fm,
\sigma_{k}=1.03
,
\eta_{\text{flat}}=4.0
and
\sigma_{\eta}=1.8

[
86
,
4
,
10
]
.

In Chapter
4
we set the lattice spacings to
\Delta x=\Delta y=\frac{1}{5}w
and
\Delta\eta_{s}=\frac{1}{5}\sigma_{\eta}
to resolve the fluctuating energy density profile (
C.31
) (or event-averaged profile). For the grid size, we set the longitudinal length to
L_{\eta}=\eta_{\text{flat}}+10\sigma_{\eta}
to fit the rapidity plateau (
C.32
). The transverse lengths
L_{x}
and
L_{y}
are automatically configured by the algorithm described in Sec. 5.2 of Ref.
[
182
]
.

C.5
Numerical implementation of second-order viscous hydrodynamics

In this appendix, we summarize how second-order viscous hydrodynamics is implemented in the
VAH
code. We evolve viscous hydrodynamics with the same numerical algorithm discussed in Sec.
4.2
except the energy-momentum tensor is decomposed as

T^{\mu\nu}=\mathcal{E}u^{\mu}u^{\nu}-(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi)\Delta^{\mu\nu}+\pi^{\mu\nu}\,,

(C.33)

where
\pi^{\mu\nu}=\Delta^{\mu\nu}_{\alpha\beta}T^{\alpha\beta}
is the shear stress tensor and
\Pi=-\frac{1}{3}\Delta_{\mu\nu}T^{\mu\nu}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}
is the bulk viscous pressure. We also define the spatial projector
\Delta^{\mu\nu}=g^{\mu\nu}-u^{\mu}u^{\nu}
and traceless double spatial projector
\Delta^{\mu\nu}_{\alpha\beta}=\frac{1}{2}(\Delta^{\mu}_{\alpha}\Delta^{\nu}_{\beta}+\Delta^{\nu}_{\beta}\Delta^{\mu}_{\alpha}-\frac{2}{3}\Delta^{\mu\nu}\Delta_{\alpha\beta})
. The corresponding dynamical variables

\bm{q}=(T^{\tau\mu},\pi^{\mu\nu},\Pi)

(C.34)

are propagated along with
\mathcal{E}
and
\bm{u}
(the mean-field
B
and anisotropic variables (
\Lambda
,
\alpha_{\perp}
,
\alpha_{L}
) are not propagated). Although
\pi^{\mu\nu}
has only five independent components, we propagate all ten components in the simulation
[
58
]
.
4
4

4

For longitudinally boost-invariant systems, we do not propagate the shear stress components
\pi^{\tau\eta}
,
\pi^{x\eta}
and
\pi^{y\eta}
.

C.5.1
Hydrodynamic equations

Here we list the evolution equations for the dynamical variables (
C.34
) (we refer the reader to Refs.
[
58
,
55
]
and App.
B.4
for details on their derivation):

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}T^{\tau\tau}+\partial_{i}(v^{i}T^{\tau\tau})=

\displaystyle-\frac{T^{\tau\tau}{+}\tau^{2}T^{\eta\eta}}{\tau}+(\pi^{\tau\tau}{-}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{-}\Pi)\partial_{i}v^{i}

(C.35a)

\displaystyle+v^{i}\partial_{i}(\pi^{\tau\tau}{-}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{-}\Pi)-\partial_{i}\pi^{\tau i}\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}T^{\tau x}+\partial_{i}(v^{i}T^{\tau x})=

\displaystyle-\frac{T^{\tau x}}{\tau}-\partial_{x}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi)+\pi^{\tau x}\partial_{i}v^{i}+v^{i}\partial_{i}\pi^{\tau x}-\partial_{i}\pi^{xi}\,,

(C.35b)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}T^{\tau y}+\partial_{i}(v^{i}T^{\tau y})=

\displaystyle-\frac{T^{\tau y}}{\tau}-\partial_{y}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi)+\pi^{\tau y}\partial_{i}v^{i}+v^{i}\partial_{i}\pi^{\tau y}-\partial_{i}\pi^{yi}\,,

(C.35c)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}T^{\tau\eta}+\partial_{i}(v^{i}T^{\tau\eta})=

\displaystyle-\frac{3T^{\tau\eta}}{\tau}-\frac{\partial_{\eta}(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi)}{\tau^{2}}+\pi^{\tau\eta}\partial_{i}v^{i}+v^{i}\partial_{i}\pi^{\tau\eta}-\partial_{i}\pi^{\eta i}\,,

(C.35d)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\pi^{\mu\nu}+\partial_{i}(v^{i}\pi^{\mu\nu})=

\displaystyle\,\pi^{\mu\nu}\partial_{i}v^{i}+\frac{1}{u^{\tau}}\left[-\frac{\pi^{\mu\nu}}{\tau_{\pi}}+\mathcal{I}_{\pi^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu}-\mathcal{P}_{\pi^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu}-\mathcal{G}_{\pi^{\prime}}^{\mu\nu}\right]\,,

(C.35e)

\displaystyle\partial_{\tau}\Pi+\partial_{i}(v^{i}\Pi)=

\displaystyle\,\Pi\partial_{i}v^{i}+\frac{1}{u^{\tau}}\left[-\frac{\Pi}{\tau_{\Pi}}+\mathcal{I}_{\Pi}\right]\,,

(C.35f)

where
T^{\eta\eta}=(\mathcal{E}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi)(u^{\eta})^{2}+(\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi)/\tau^{2}+\pi^{\eta\eta}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\mu\nu}_{\pi^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\,2\beta_{\pi}\sigma^{\mu\nu}+\Delta^{\mu\nu}_{\alpha\beta}\big(2\pi^{\lambda(\alpha}\omega^{\beta)}_{\,\,\,\lambda}-\bar{\tau}_{\pi\pi}\pi^{\lambda(\alpha}\sigma^{\beta)}_{\,\,\,\lambda}\big)-\bar{\delta}_{\pi\pi}\pi^{\mu\nu}\theta+\bar{\lambda}_{\pi\Pi}\Pi\sigma^{\mu\nu}\,,

(C.36a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{\Pi}=

\displaystyle\,-\beta_{\Pi}\theta-\bar{\delta}_{\Pi\Pi}\Pi\theta+\bar{\lambda}_{\Pi\pi}\pi^{\mu\nu}\sigma_{\mu\nu}\,,

(C.36b)

are the gradient source terms for
\pi^{\mu\nu}
and
\Pi
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\mu\nu}_{\pi^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\,\left(\pi^{\mu\alpha}u^{\nu}+\pi^{\nu\alpha}u^{\mu}\right)a_{\alpha}\,,

(C.37a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{\mu\nu}_{\pi^{\prime}}=

\displaystyle\,u^{\gamma}\Gamma^{\mu}_{\gamma\lambda}\pi^{\nu\lambda}+u^{\gamma}\Gamma^{\nu}_{\gamma\lambda}\pi^{\mu\lambda}\,,

(C.37b)

are the spatial projection and geometric source terms for
\pi^{\mu\nu}
. We also define the velocity-shear tensor
\sigma^{\mu\nu}=\Delta^{\mu\nu}_{\alpha\beta}D^{(\alpha}u^{\beta)}
and vorticity tensor
\omega^{\mu\nu}=\Delta^{\mu}_{\alpha}\Delta^{\nu}_{\beta}D^{[\alpha}u^{\beta]}
. The transport coefficients are listed in App.
B.4
.

The components of
\sigma^{\mu\nu}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\mu\nu}_{\pi^{\prime}}
are the same as
\sigma_{\perp}^{\mu\nu}
and
\mathcal{G}^{\mu\nu}_{\pi}
after replacing
\Xi^{\mu\nu}_{\alpha\beta}\to\Delta^{\mu\nu}_{\alpha\beta}
and
\pi^{\mu\nu}_{\perp}\to\pi^{\mu\nu}
in Eqs. (
C.16
) and (
C.23
), respectively. The components of
\omega^{\mu\nu}
are

\omega^{\mu\nu}=D^{[\mu}u^{\nu]}-\frac{u^{\mu}a^{\nu}{-}u^{\nu}a^{\mu}}{2}\,,

(C.38)

where
D^{[\mu}u^{\nu]}
is given by Eq. (
C.20
).

C.5.2
Reconstructing the energy density and fluid velocity

We reconstruct the energy density by solving the following nonlinear equation via Newton’s method
[
24
,
58
]
:

f(\mathcal{E})=0\,,

(C.39)

where

f(\mathcal{E})=\left(\bar{M}^{\tau}{-}\mathcal{E}\right)\left(\bar{M}^{\tau}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi\right)-(\bar{M}^{x})^{2}-(\bar{M}^{y})^{2}-(\tau\bar{M}^{\eta})^{2}\,,

(C.40)

with
\bar{M}^{\mu}=T^{\tau\mu}-\pi^{\tau\mu}
and
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}=\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}(\mathcal{E})
being the QCD equation of state. Using the previous energy density for the initial guess, we iterate the solution to Eq. (
C.39
) as

\mathcal{E}\leftarrow\mathcal{E}-\frac{f(\mathcal{E})}{df/d\mathcal{E}}\,,

(C.41)

where

\frac{df}{d\mathcal{E}}=c_{s}^{2}(\bar{M}^{\tau}{-}\mathcal{E})-\bar{M}^{\tau}-\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}-\Pi\,,

(C.42)

with
c_{s}^{2}=d\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}/d\mathcal{E}
being the QCD speed of sound squared. We repeat the iteration (
C.41
) until we achieve sufficient convergence or the energy density falls below
\mathcal{E}_{\text{min}}
. If the bulk viscous pressure
\Pi<-\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}
, we regulate it so that
\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}+\Pi=0
; this allows us to solve for
\mathcal{E}
explicitly
[
24
]
:

\mathcal{E}=\bar{M}^{\tau}-\frac{(\bar{M}^{x})^{2}{+}(\bar{M}^{y})^{2}{+}(\tau\bar{M}^{\eta})^{2}}{{\bar{M}}^{\tau}}\,.

(C.43)

Afterwards, we regulate the energy density via Eq. (
4.59
) and evaluate the fluid velocity components as

\displaystyle u^{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{M}^{x}}{\sqrt{\left(\mathcal{E}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi\right)\left(\bar{M}^{\tau}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi\right)}}\,,

(C.44a)

\displaystyle u^{y}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{M}^{y}}{\sqrt{\left(\mathcal{E}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi\right)\left(\bar{M}^{\tau}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi\right)}}\,,

(C.44b)

\displaystyle u^{\eta}

\displaystyle=\frac{\bar{M}^{\eta}}{\sqrt{\left(\mathcal{E}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi\right)\left(\bar{M}^{\tau}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}{+}\Pi\right)}}\,.

(C.44c)

C.5.3
Regulating the shear stress and bulk viscous pressure

In this regulation scheme, we first adjust the shear stress components

\displaystyle\pi^{\eta\eta}\leftarrow

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{\tau^{2}\left(1{+}u_{\perp}^{2}\right)}\Big[\pi^{xx}\big((u^{x})^{2}{-}(u^{\tau})^{2}\big)+\pi^{yy}\big((u^{y})^{2}{-}(u^{\tau})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+2\big(\pi^{xy}u^{x}u^{y}+\tau^{2}(\pi^{x\eta}u^{x}{+}\pi^{y\eta}u^{y})u^{\eta}\big)\Big]\,,

(C.45a)

\displaystyle\pi^{\tau x}\leftarrow

\displaystyle\,\frac{\pi^{xx}u^{x}+\pi^{xy}u^{y}+\tau^{2}\pi^{x\eta}u^{\eta}}{u^{\tau}}\,,

(C.45b)

\displaystyle\pi^{\tau y}\leftarrow

\displaystyle\,\frac{\pi^{xy}u^{x}+\pi^{yy}u^{y}+\tau^{2}\pi^{y\eta}u^{\eta}}{u^{\tau}}\,,

(C.45c)

\displaystyle\pi^{\tau\eta}\leftarrow

\displaystyle\,\frac{\pi^{x\eta}u^{x}+\pi^{y\eta}u^{y}+\tau^{2}\pi^{\eta\eta}u^{\eta}}{u^{\tau}}\,,

(C.45d)

\displaystyle\pi^{\tau\tau}\leftarrow

\displaystyle\,\frac{\pi^{\tau x}u^{x}+\pi^{\tau y}u^{y}+\tau^{2}\pi^{\tau\eta}u^{\eta}}{u^{\tau}}\,,

(C.45e)

so that
\pi^{\mu\nu}
satisfies the orthogonality and tracelessness conditions

\displaystyle\pi^{\mu\nu}u_{\nu}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(C.46a)

\displaystyle\pi^{\mu}_{\mu}

\displaystyle=0\,.

(C.46b)

Then we regulate the shear stress and bulk viscous pressure as

\displaystyle\pi^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle\leftarrow\gamma_{\text{reg}}\pi^{\mu\nu}\,,

(C.47a)

\displaystyle\Pi

\displaystyle\leftarrow\gamma_{\text{reg}}\Pi\,,

(C.47b)

where

\gamma_{\text{reg}}=\min\Bigg(1,\sqrt{\frac{3\mathcal{P}_{\text{eq}}^{2}}{\pi{\,\cdot\,}\pi+3\Pi^{2}}}\Bigg)\,,

(C.48)

with
\pi{\,\cdot\,}\pi=\pi_{{\mu\nu}}\pi^{\mu\nu}
. The regulation factor
\gamma_{\text{reg}}
usually suppresses
\pi^{\mu\nu}
and
\Pi
around the edges of the fireball at early times
\tau<1
fm/
c
, especially in standard viscous hydrodynamics (e.g. see Figs. (
4.19
c) and (
4.20
c)).

Appendix D

D.1
Moments of the distribution function

We define the thermal, isotropic and anisotropic integrals that appear in Chapter
5
. For each species
n
let

J_{kq,n}\equiv\int_{p}\frac{(u\cdot p)^{k-2q}({-}p{\,\cdot\,}\Delta{\,\cdot\,}p)^{q}}{(2q{+}1)!!}f_{\mathrm{eq},n}\bar{f}_{\mathrm{eq},n}\,.

(D.1)

The
thermal integrals
over the local-equilibrium distribution are then given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{kq}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}J_{kq,n}\,,

(D.2a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}_{kq}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}b_{n}J_{kq,n}\,,

(D.2b)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{kq}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}b^{2}_{n}J_{kq,n}\,,

(D.2c)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}_{kq}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}m_{n}^{2}J_{kq,n}\,,

(D.2d)

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{kq}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n}b_{n}m_{n}^{2}J_{kq,n}\,.

(D.2e)

The
isotropic integrals
over the PTB distribution (
5.38
) without shear stress modifications (i.e. for
\pi_{ij}=0
) are

\mathcal{L}_{kq}=\sum_{n}\int_{p}\frac{(u\cdot p)^{k-2q}({-}p{\,\cdot\,}\Delta{\,\cdot\,}p)^{q}}{(2q{+}1)!!}f_{\lambda,n}\,,

(D.3)

where

f_{\lambda,n}=\frac{g_{n}(1{+}\lambda_{\Pi})^{-3}}{\exp\left[\dfrac{1}{T}\sqrt{m_{n}^{2}-\dfrac{p{\,\cdot\,}\Delta{\,\cdot\,}p}{(1{+}\lambda_{\Pi})^{2}}}\right]+\Theta_{n}}\,.

(D.4)

In particular, the modified energy density and isotropic pressure are given by
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}(\lambda_{\Pi},z_{\Pi},T)=z_{\Pi}\mathcal{L}_{20}(\lambda_{\Pi},T)
and
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}(\lambda_{\Pi},z_{\Pi},T)=z_{\Pi}\mathcal{L}_{21}(\lambda_{\Pi},T)
. Finally, the
anisotropic integrals
are given by

\mathcal{J}^{(R)}_{krqs}=\sum_{n}\frac{1}{(2q)!!}\int_{p}(u\cdot p)^{k{-}r{-}2q}(-Z{\,\cdot\,}p)^{r}({-}p{\,\cdot\,}\Xi{\,\cdot\,}p)^{q}(p\cdot\Omega\cdot p)^{s/2}f_{a,n}\bar{f}_{a,n}\,.

(D.5)

D.2
Conservation laws in first-order approximation

Here we derive the expression for the time derivatives (
5.15
) by making a first-order approximation to the conversation laws (
5.14
). The conservation equations for the net baryon number and energy up to first order in gradients are

\dot{n}_{B}=-n_{B}\theta,\qquad\dot{\mathcal{E}}=-(\mathcal{E}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}})\theta\,.

(D.6)

Taking the time derivative of the kinetic definitions for the net baryon and energy densities,

\!\!n_{B}=\sum_{n}b_{n}\int_{p}(u\cdot p)f_{\mathrm{eq},n},\quad\mathcal{E}=\sum_{n}\int_{p}(u\cdot p)^{2}f_{\mathrm{eq},n},\!\!

(D.7)

one obtains

\dot{\alpha}_{B}=\mathcal{G}(T,\alpha_{B})\theta\,,\qquad\dot{T}=\mathcal{F}(T,\alpha_{B})\theta\,.

(D.8)

The coefficients
\mathcal{G}
and
\mathcal{F}
appear in the RTA Chapman–Enskog expansion (
5.17
):

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}

\displaystyle=T\left(\frac{(\mathcal{E}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}})\mathcal{N}_{20}-n_{B}\mathcal{J}_{30}}{\mathcal{J}_{30}\mathcal{M}_{10}-\mathcal{N}_{20}^{2}}\right)\,,

(D.9a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}

\displaystyle=T^{2}\left(\frac{n_{B}\mathcal{N}_{20}-(\mathcal{E}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}})\mathcal{M}_{10}}{\mathcal{J}_{30}\mathcal{M}_{10}-\mathcal{N}_{20}^{2}}\right)\,.

(D.9b)

The evolution equation for the fluid velocity up to first-order in gradients is given by

\dot{u}^{\mu}=\frac{\nabla^{\mu}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}}{\mathcal{E}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}}\,.

(D.10)

After computing the spatial gradient of the equilibrium pressure

\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}=\frac{1}{3}\sum_{n}\int_{p}(-p{\,\cdot\,}\Delta{\,\cdot\,}p)f_{\mathrm{eq},n}\,,

(D.11)

Eq. (
D.10
) can be rewritten as

\dot{u}^{\mu}=\nabla^{\mu}{\ln}T+\frac{n_{B}T}{\mathcal{E}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}}}\nabla^{\mu}\alpha_{B}\,,

(D.12)

where we made use of the identities
[
65
]

\mathcal{J}_{31}=(\mathcal{E}{+}\mathcal{P}_{\mathrm{eq}})T
and
\mathcal{N}_{21}=n_{B}T
.

Appendix E

E.1
Expansion of the hydrodynamic generator

In this section we show that the correspondence between the hydrodynamic generator and Borel resummed RTA Chapman–Enskog series holds for all orders in the Knudsen number. We thank Chandrodoy Chattopadhyay for first working out the proof in the Bjorken case; we further generalize it to (3+1)–dimensions.

E.1.1
(0+1)–d hydrodynamic generator

For Bjorken flow, we want to prove the map

f_{\text{G}}(\tau,p)=\int^{\tau}_{\tau_{0}}\frac{d\tau^{\prime}D(\tau,\tau^{\prime})f_{\text{eq}}(\tau^{\prime},p)}{\tau_{r}(\tau^{\prime})}\to{\int^{\infty}_{0}}dz\,e^{-z}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{{z^{n}}{\left[{-}\tau_{r}(\tau)\partial_{\tau}\right]^{n}}f_{\text{eq}}(\tau,p)}{n!}\,,

(E.1)

given that the particle interaction measure
z_{0}\to\infty
at late times. We will work with the expression given by the coordinate transformation in Eq. (
8.16
):

f_{\text{G}}(\tau,p)={\int^{z_{0}}_{0}}dz\,e^{-z}f_{\text{eq}}\big(h^{-1}(z,\tau),p\big)\,,

(E.2)

where

z=h(\tau^{\prime},\tau)=\int^{\tau}_{\tau^{\prime}}\frac{d\tau^{\prime\prime}}{\tau_{r}(\tau^{\prime\prime})}

(E.3)

and

\tau^{\prime}=h^{-1}(z,\tau)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c_{n}(\tau)\,z^{n}\,.

(E.4)

First we show the recurrence relation (
8.21
) between the expansion coefficients
c_{n}
:

\displaystyle c_{0}

\displaystyle=\tau\,,

(E.5a)

\displaystyle c_{n}

\displaystyle=-\frac{\tau_{r}(\tau)\partial_{\tau}c_{n{-}1}}{n}\indent\forall\,n\geq 1\,.

(E.5b)

We apply the partial derivative
\partial_{\tau}
on both sides of Eq. (
E.4
) (while keeping
\tau^{\prime}=
const):

0=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\partial_{\tau}c_{n}\,z^{n}+\frac{nc_{n}\,z^{n-1}}{\tau_{r}(\tau)}\right)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n}\left(\partial_{\tau}c_{n}+\frac{(n{+}1)c_{n+1}}{\tau_{r}(\tau)}\right)\,,

(E.6)

where we used the relation
\partial_{\tau}z=1/\tau_{r}(\tau)
and incremented the index
n
in the second term. Setting the expression in parentheses to zero gives us Eq. (
E.5
b). We compute the coefficient
c_{0}
by setting
z=0
in Eq. (
E.4
) so that
c_{0}=\tau^{\prime}
. From Eq. (
E.3
),
\tau^{\prime}=\tau
when
z=0
, thus establishing the recurrence relation (
E.5
).

Next we expand the function
f_{\text{eq}}\big(h^{-1}(z,\tau),p\big)
in the integrand (
E.2
) as a power series:

f_{\text{eq}}\big(h^{-1}(z,\tau),p\big)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}(\tau,p)z^{n}

(E.7)

and compute the coefficients
b_{n}
. With the recurrence relation (
E.5
), we can construct the identity

\left(\frac{\partial h^{-1}}{\partial z}\right)_{\tau}\,+\,\tau_{r}(\tau)\left(\frac{\partial h^{-1}}{\partial\tau}\right)_{z}=0\,.

(E.8)

Thus, after the applying the chain rule on
f_{\text{eq}}\big(h^{-1}(z,\tau),p\big)
, we have

\left(\frac{\partial f_{\text{eq}}(h^{-1},p)}{\partial z}\right)_{\tau}\,+\,\tau_{r}(\tau)\left(\frac{\partial f_{\text{eq}}(h^{-1},p)}{\partial\tau}\right)_{z}=0\,.

(E.9)

Inserting the power series (
E.7
) into Eq. (
E.9
) yields the recurrence relation

b_{n}=-\frac{\tau_{r}(\tau)\partial_{\tau}b_{n-1}}{n}\indent\forall\,n\geq 1\,,

(E.10)

which is identical to Eq. (
E.5
b). By setting
z=0
in Eq. (
E.7
), we get the first coefficient

b_{0}=f_{\text{eq}}(\tau,p)\,.

(E.11)

As a result, the series expansion of the hydrodynamic generator (
E.2
) becomes

f_{\text{G}}(\tau,p)={\int^{z_{0}}_{0}}dz\,e^{-z}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{{z^{n}}{\left[{-}\tau_{r}(\tau)\partial_{\tau}\right]^{n}}f_{\text{eq}}(\tau,p)}{n!}\,.

(E.12)

Finally, we take the limit
z_{0}\to\infty
to recover the Borel resummed RTA Chapman–Enskog series (
8.10
).

E.1.2
(3+1)–d hydrodynamic generator

The generalization of the correspondence to (3+1)–dimensions

\begin{split}f_{\text{G}}(x,p)&=\int^{x}_{x_{-}}{dx^{\prime}{\cdot\,}s^{-1}(x^{\prime},p)D(x,x^{\prime},p)f_{\text{eq}}(x^{\prime},p)}\\
&\to\int_{0}^{\infty}dz\,e^{-z}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{z^{n}\left[-s^{\mu}(x,p)\partial_{\mu}\right]^{n}f_{\text{eq}}(x,p)}{n!}\end{split}

(E.13)

is completely analogous. We start from Eq. (
8.68
)

f_{\text{G}}(x,p)=\int_{0}^{z_{-}}dz\,e^{-z}f_{\text{eq}}\big(h^{-1}(z,x,p),p\big)\,,

(E.14)

where

z=h(x^{\prime},x,p)=\int^{x}_{x^{\prime}}dx^{\prime\prime}{\cdot\,}s^{-1}(x^{\prime\prime},p)

(E.15)

and

x^{\prime}_{\mu}=h^{-1}_{\mu}(z,x,p)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}c_{n,\mu}(x,p)\,z^{n}\,.

(E.16)

After taking the directional derivative
s^{\nu}(x,p)\partial_{\nu}
of Eq. (
E.16
) while keeping the coordinate
x^{\prime}_{\mu}
fixed, we get

0=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n}\left(s^{\nu}(x,p)\partial_{\nu}c_{n,\mu}+(n{+}1)c_{n+1,\mu}\right)\,,

(E.17)

where we used the identity
s^{\nu}(x,p)\partial_{\nu}z=1
. This gives us the recurrence relation (
8.75
) (we get
c_{0,\mu}=x_{\mu}
by setting
z=0
in Eqs. (
E.15
) – (
E.16
))).

Then we expand the function in Eq. (
E.14
) as a power series:

f_{\text{eq}}\big(h^{-1}(z,x,p),p\big)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}(x,p)z^{n}\,.

(E.18)

From the recurrence relation (
8.75
), we have the identity

\left(\frac{\partial h^{-1}_{\mu}}{\partial z}\right)_{x}\,+\,s^{\nu}(x,p)\left(\frac{\partial h^{-1}_{\mu}}{\partial x^{\nu}}\right)_{z}=0\,,

(E.19)

or equivalently

\left(\frac{\partial f_{\text{eq}}(h^{-1},p)}{\partial z}\right)_{x}\,+\,s^{\nu}(x,p)\left(\frac{\partial f_{\text{eq}}(h^{-1},p)}{\partial x^{\nu}}\right)_{z}=0\,.

(E.20)

After inserting the power series (
E.18
) into Eq. (
E.20
), we find the recurrence relation

b_{n}=-\frac{s^{\nu}(x,p)\partial_{\nu}b_{n-1}}{n}\indent\forall\,n\geq 1\,,

(E.21)

while the first coefficient is (after setting
z=0
in Eq. (
E.18
))

b_{0}=f_{\text{eq}}(x,p)\,.

(E.22)

Therefore, the expansion of the (3+1)–d hydrodynamic generator (
E.14
) simplifies to

f_{\text{G}}(x,p)=\int_{0}^{z_{-}}dz\,e^{-z}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{z^{n}\left[-s^{\mu}(x,p)\partial_{\mu}\right]^{n}f_{\text{eq}}(x,p)}{n!}

(E.23)

After taking the limit
z_{-}\to\infty
, we arrive at Eq. (
8.66
).

E.2
Standard gradient corrections

Here we compute the standard gradient corrections to the normalized shear stress for a conformal system undergoing Bjorken expansion:

\bar{\pi}_{s}=\bar{\pi}_{s}^{(1)}+\bar{\pi}_{s}^{(2)}+\bar{\pi}_{s}^{(3)}+O(\text{Kn}^{4})\,.

(E.24)

Before proceeding, we make a change in variables
w=\tau^{2}p^{\eta}
to rewrite the local-equilibrium distribution (
8.5
) as

f_{\text{eq}}(\tau,p_{T},w)=\exp\Bigg[-\frac{\sqrt{\tau^{2}p_{\perp}^{2}+w^{2}}}{\tau T(\tau)}\Bigg]\,.

(E.25)

The first-order gradient correction to the distribution function is

\delta f^{(1)}=-\tau_{r}\partial_{\tau}f_{\text{eq}}=-\frac{\tau_{\pi}f_{\text{eq}}}{\tau^{2}T}\bigg(\frac{w^{2}}{v}\,+\,v\tau\partial_{\tau}\ln T\bigg)\,,

(E.26)

where we set
\tau_{r}=\tau_{\pi}=5(\eta/\mathcal{S})/T
(taking
\eta/\mathcal{S}
as a constant) and
v=\sqrt{\tau^{2}p_{\perp}^{2}+w^{2}}
. The first-order correction to the shear stress is then

\bar{\pi}^{(1)}_{s}=\frac{2\pi^{2}}{3T^{4}}\int_{p}\left(\frac{p_{\perp}^{2}}{2}-\frac{w^{2}}{\tau^{2}}\right)\delta f^{(1)}\,,

(E.27)

where
\int_{p}=\int\dfrac{d^{2}p_{\perp}dw}{v(2\pi)^{3}}
. After inserting
\delta f^{(1)}
in Eq. (
E.27
) and substituting the spherical coordinates

\displaystyle\tau p^{x}

\displaystyle=v\sin\theta\cos\phi\,,

(E.28a)

\displaystyle\tau p^{y}

\displaystyle=v\sin\theta\sin\phi\,,

(E.28b)

\displaystyle w

\displaystyle=v\cos\theta\,,

(E.28c)

one obtains

\bar{\pi}^{(1)}_{s}=\frac{16\tau_{\pi}}{15\tau}\,.

(E.29)

The
\delta f^{(2)}
and
\delta f^{(3)}
corrections are too cumbersome to list here. We simply state the results for the second and third-order shear corrections (for the derivation see the auxiliary materials available in the link referenced in footnote
8
):

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}^{(2)}_{s}

\displaystyle=-\frac{16\tau_{\pi}^{2}}{105\tau^{2}}\left(15+49\tau\partial_{\tau}{\ln T}\right)\,,

(E.30a)

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}^{(3)}_{s}

\displaystyle=\frac{16\tau_{\pi}^{3}}{105\tau^{3}}\big(\tau\partial_{\tau}{\ln T}(135+182\tau\partial_{\tau}{\ln T})+77\tau^{2}\partial^{2}_{\tau}{\ln T}\big)\,.

(E.30b)

Here we used the relation
\partial_{\tau}\tau_{\pi}=-\tau_{\pi}\partial_{\tau}{\ln T}
to eliminate time derivatives of the shear relaxation time. At late times, the gradients
\text{Kn}=\tau_{\pi}/\tau\sim\tau^{-2/3}
become small. Hence, the asymptotic solutions for the energy conservation law and its time derivative (
8.35
) are

\displaystyle\tau\,\partial_{\tau}{\ln T}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{3}+\frac{4\tau_{\pi}}{45\tau}+O\big(\text{Kn}^{2}\big)\,,

(E.31a)

\displaystyle\tau^{2}\partial^{2}_{\tau}{\ln T}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3}+O\left(\text{Kn}\right)\,.

(E.31b)

The shear corrections (
E.30
) then reduce to

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}^{(2)}_{s}

\displaystyle\approx\frac{64\tau_{\pi}^{2}}{315\tau^{2}}-\frac{448\tau_{\pi}^{3}}{675\tau^{3}}\,,

(E.32a)

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}^{(3)}_{s}

\displaystyle\approx\frac{128\tau_{\pi}^{3}}{945\tau^{3}}\,.

(E.32b)

Finally, the non-hydrodynamic modes in the exact solution (
8.29
) decay at late times since
z_{0}\sim\tau^{2/3}
. Using this, the second and third-order corrections in Eq. (
E.32
) can be regrouped as

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}^{(2)}_{s}

\displaystyle\to\frac{64\tau_{\pi}^{2}}{315\tau^{2}}\,,

(E.33a)

\displaystyle\bar{\pi}^{(3)}_{s}

\displaystyle\to-\frac{832\tau_{\pi}^{3}}{1575\tau^{3}}\,,

(E.33b)

which appear in the Burnett and super-Burnett solutions (
8.36
b,c).

E.3
Leading non-hydrodynamic mode correction

Here we compute the shear stress correction from the leading non-hydrodynamic mode
\delta f_{\text{G}}^{(0)}=e^{-z_{0}}(f_{0}-f_{\text{eq}})
:

\bar{\pi}^{(0)}_{s,\text{G}}=\frac{2\pi^{2}e^{-z_{0}}}{3T^{4}}\int_{p}\left(\frac{p_{\perp}^{2}}{2}-\frac{w^{2}}{\tau^{2}}\right)f_{0}(\tau_{0},p_{T},w)\,,

(E.34)

where the second term
\propto f_{\text{eq}}(\tau,p_{T},w)
vanishes by symmetry. The code
[
101
,
159
,
218
]
that evolves the RTA Bjorken solution (
8.8
) gives the user the option to initialize the distribution as

f_{0}(\tau_{0},p_{T},w)=\exp\left[-\frac{\sqrt{\tau_{0}^{2}p_{\perp}^{2}+(1{+}\xi_{0})w^{2}}}{\tau_{0}\Lambda_{0}}\right]\,,

(E.35)

where

\Lambda_{0}=T_{0}\,\mathcal{H}\big((1{+}\xi_{0})^{-1/2}\big)^{-1/4}

(E.36)

is the effective temperature and
\xi_{0}
is the momentum anisotropy parameter. After substituting the spherical coordinates

\displaystyle\tau_{0}p^{x}

\displaystyle=v_{0}\sin\theta\cos\phi\,,

(E.37a)

\displaystyle\tau_{0}p^{y}

\displaystyle=v_{0}\sin\theta\sin\phi\,,

(E.37b)

\displaystyle(1+\xi_{0})^{1/2}w

\displaystyle=v_{0}\cos\theta\,,

(E.37c)

where
v_{0}=\sqrt{\tau_{0}^{2}p_{\perp}^{2}+(1{+}\xi_{0})w^{2}}
, Eq. (
E.34
) can be rewritten as

\bar{\pi}^{(0)}_{s,\text{G}}=e^{-z_{0}}\frac{\Lambda^{4}_{0}}{T^{4}}\left[\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{H}_{\perp}\Big(\frac{\tau_{0}\alpha_{L,0}}{\tau}\Big){-}\mathcal{H}_{L}\Big(\frac{\tau_{0}\alpha_{L,0}}{\tau}\Big)\right]\,,

(E.38)

where
\alpha_{L,0}=(1\,{+}\,\xi_{0})^{-1/2}
and the hypergeometric functions
[
101
]

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\perp}(x)

\displaystyle=\,x\int_{{-}1}^{1}\frac{d{\cos\theta}\,(1-\cos^{2}\theta)}{\sqrt{1+(x^{2}{-}1)\cos^{2}\theta}}\,,

(E.39a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{L}(x)

\displaystyle=\,x^{3}\int_{{-}1}^{1}\frac{d{\cos\theta}\,\cos^{2}\theta}{\sqrt{1+(x^{2}{-}1)\cos^{2}\theta}}

(E.39b)

are

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\perp}(x)

\displaystyle=\,\frac{1}{1{-}x^{2}}\big(x^{2}+(1{-}2x^{2})\,t(x^{-2}{-}1)\big)\,,

(E.40a)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{L}(x)

\displaystyle=\,\frac{x^{2}}{1{-}x^{2}}\big({-}x^{2}+t(x^{-2}{-}1)\big)\,,

(E.40b)

with
t(y)=\tan^{-1}{\sqrt{y}}/\sqrt{y}
. In Sec.
8.2
, we had initialized the shear stress to
\pi_{s}(\tau_{0})=0
so that
\xi_{0}=0
and
\Lambda_{0}=T_{0}
. Then Eq. (
E.38
) reduces to Eq. (
8.34
a).

E.4
Solution of the RTA Boltzmann equation in (3+1)–dimensions

Here we derive the (3+1)–dimensional solution of the RTA Boltzmann equation in Minkowski spacetime. First, we multiply both sides of Eq. (
8.1
) by the function

q(x,p)=\exp\left[{\int_{x_{\star}}^{x}}dx^{\prime\prime}{\cdot\,}s^{-1}(x^{\prime\prime},p)\right]\,,

(E.41)

where the path integral runs over a straight line that is parallel to
p^{\mu}
; the coordinate
x_{\star}^{\mu}
is a fixed point on the characteristic line (see Figure
8.4
)

{x^{\prime}}^{\mu}(\lambda^{\prime})=x^{\mu}-\lambda^{\prime}p^{\mu}\indent\indent 0\leq\lambda^{\prime}\leq\lambda_{-}\,,

(E.42)

with
\lambda_{-}=(t-t_{-})/E
. Equation (
8.1
) can be rewritten as

s^{\mu}(x,p)\partial_{\mu}\left[q(x,p)f(x,p)\right]=q(x,p)f_{\text{eq}}(x,p)\,.

(E.43)

Now we integrate this equation along the characteristic line (
E.42
):

\int_{x_{-}}^{x}dx^{\prime}{\cdot\,}s^{-1}(x^{\prime},p)\,s^{\mu}(x^{\prime},p)\frac{\partial\left[q(x^{\prime},p)f(x^{\prime},p)\right]}{\partial{x^{\prime}}^{\mu}}=\int_{x_{-}}^{x}dx^{\prime}{\cdot\,}s^{-1}(x^{\prime},p)\,q(x^{\prime},p)f_{\text{eq}}(x^{\prime},p)\,.

(E.44)

The l.h.s. of Eq. (
E.44
) can be parameterized in terms of
\lambda^{\prime}
:

\int_{\lambda_{-}}^{0}d\lambda^{\prime}\frac{d\left[q(x^{\prime}(\lambda^{\prime}),p)f(x^{\prime}(\lambda^{\prime}),p)\right]}{d\lambda^{\prime}}=q(x,p)f(x,p)-q(x_{-},p)f(x_{-},p)\,,

(E.45)

where we used the relations
d{x^{\prime}}^{\nu}=-p^{\nu}d\lambda^{\prime}
and
\dfrac{\partial}{\partial{x^{\prime}}^{\mu}}=-\dfrac{p_{\mu}}{p^{2}}\dfrac{d}{d\lambda^{\prime}}
. For the distribution function on the hypersurface
\Sigma_{-}
(see Figure
8.4
) we take

f(x_{-},p)=f_{0}(x_{-},p)\Theta(t_{0}-t_{-})\,.

(E.46)

The solution of the RTA Boltzmann equation is then

f(x,p)=\,\frac{q(x_{-},p)}{q(x,p)}f_{0}(x_{-},p)\Theta(t_{0}-t_{-})\,+\,\int_{x_{-}}^{x}dx^{\prime}{\cdot\,}s^{-1}(x^{\prime},p)\,\frac{q(x^{\prime},p)}{q(x,p)}f_{\text{eq}}(x^{\prime},p)\,.

(E.47)

After using the identity
D(x_{2},x_{1},p)=q(x_{1},p)/q(x_{2},p)
, one arrives at Eq. (
8.82
).
